- Skins keep their look; the controller decides the symbols. A skin's own glyphs only apply to the matching kind of controller: a skin's PlayStation glyphs (✕ ○ □ △) only show with a PlayStation pad, with an Xbox pad it shows A B X Y.
- Security: read-only HID, opened shared (games and Steam keep reading the pad), no driver, no administrator rights, only devices with Sony's vendor id in their path are opened at all, no feature reports, no rumble and no light bar (nothing is ever written to the pad). Reports are read with a fixed size and bounds; gyro and touchpad data are not evaluated and never leave the process. PS cannot do anything Guide could not already do.

**Battery of Xbox pads over Bluetooth**
- When XInput reports no battery for a connected Xbox pad (often over Bluetooth, or because Steam Input puts its virtual pad in the slot), CouchHUD also asks Windows.Gaming.Input and the guide menu shows bars and a percent ("🎮 1 ▮▮▯ 64 %") instead of just "🎮 1". Because Windows does not tell which XInput slot is which pad, the value is only used when that is unambiguous: exactly one pad reports a battery there, no Xbox pad already knows its battery through XInput, and a charging value does not count next to a pad on its cable; it then goes to the one real pad without a level, only when there is none to the one Steam virtual pad (not next to a pad on its cable); with two candidates nothing changes. PlayStation pads and their mirrors are left alone. A charging pad never counts as "battery low"; without a battery (on the cable) it keeps the plug symbol.
- Security: only reads the battery state of local controllers through a Windows API, nothing is written, no new rights. Failures of Windows.Gaming.Input are caught, XInput keeps working as before.

**Screenshots with the controller**
- **Hold Guide + Y** takes a screenshot, also in a game; *Screenshot* in the Guide menu first closes the menu (a running game comes back to the front) and then captures. CouchHUD captures by itself (DXGI desktop duplication, else GDI), the screen the game or CouchHUD is on, no longer through the Game Bar; clips and recording stay with the Game Bar.
- Saved as PNG to `%USERPROFILE%\Pictures\CouchHUD\Screenshots\<game or CouchHUD>_<time>.png`. A short message with a preview (over a game a small window on top, in CouchHUD with a camera sound when sounds are on), an honest message when it fails (also for an all-black picture). The gallery shows the screenshots first.
- Guide + Y does not collide with Steam's Guide chords (RB screenshot, X keyboard). With the combination on, a tap on Guide acts on release; holding opens the Guide menu as before. Known limits: the game sees the Y press too (XInput is shared); when the Game Bar or Steam owns Guide, their overlay may end up in the picture.
- Setting *Settings › Controller › Screenshot with Guide + Y* (expert mode, on).
- Security: local only, never uploaded, not reachable from MCP, plugins or the assistant (the combination reads the real controller only), no new service operation. The phone can drive the Guide menu but cannot take a screenshot (when the last button came from the phone, CouchHUD refuses) and never sees a picture. No screenshot while a code entry (couch, parents', profile code, Windows sign-in), a secret field of the on-screen keyboard or the phone's pairing code is shown, at most one per second. CouchHUD's own messages are closed before the capture; a half-written file goes away with its handle. The file name from the game's name is cleaned (no path signs, no device names, at most 60 characters); a folder link below *Pictures* stops the save, the file is always created new and its real path checked. Allowed under parental controls and on a locked couch.

**Switchable areas**
- Games, apps, browser and news can be switched off one by one, for a plain app and browser launcher for example: in the setup assistant (a new step after the terms) and under *Settings › HUD › Areas*. Everything stays on by default, one of games, apps and browser always stays on.
- An area that is off disappears completely: tab, tiles, Guide-menu rows, settings and assistant steps, and its background work (for games: store scans, art, store match, save backups with their notifications, game downloads, game clients in the background, Steam settings). The host refuses its calls, also for the assistant and MCP. The data is kept and comes back when the area is on again.
- One table in Core (`FeatureAreas`) assigns every setting, call, tool and background service to an area or "always"; tests check that no setting key is missing.
- Changing it only unlocked, with parental controls with the parents' code, never on a locked couch, never through the remote, MCP, plugins or the assistant. With parental controls on, games are still recognised and counted with the area off.
**Age limit by USK, PEGI or ESRB**
- The parental age limit no longer goes by the USK only: CouchHUD reads every age rating from the Steam store (USK, PEGI, ESRB, as a fallback also ClassInd and CERO) and takes the Windows region's system automatically (Germany USK, USA and Canada ESRB, the rest of Europe PEGI, else the strictest rating). In expert mode it can be fixed under *Parental controls › Age ratings by*.
- The age limit picker offers the active system's steps (USK 6/12/16/18, PEGI 3/7/12/16/18, ESRB E/E10+/T/M; there is no ESRB AO step, so an 18 never lets every M game through, AO games stay blocked at every step), the game page shows the rating in it ("PEGI 16", "ESRB T"). When the system has no rating for a game, the strictest of the others counts, then Steam's required age, else the game counts as without an age rating.
- Switching the system moves every age limit of every profile to the nearest step below, never looser. A stored 18 counts as M under the ESRB. Existing installs with an age limit stay on the USK (as it counted before) and do not silently get looser by the region; store data of older versions keeps working. When the Windows region is switched to another system under "Automatic", every game gets the stricter rating of the saved and the new system until the parents save the limit or the system again; a hint in parental controls shows this.
- Security: a store rating that cannot be read never counts as mild but as unknown. Switching the system works only unlocked, not with a broken `config.json`, and with parental controls only with the parents' code for exactly this action. An unknown system in the file counts as the strictest rating, a limit off the steps as the strictest step.

**Freezing the game: never with anti-cheat**
- "Pause game while CouchHUD is in front" stays off by default and in expert mode, with a clear warning: a frozen game can look like tampering to an anti-cheat and get the account banned.
- CouchHUD freezes no game with a detected anti-cheat (Easy Anti-Cheat, BattlEye, Vanguard, GameGuard, XIGNCODE, PunkBuster, Ricochet, mhyprot, ACE, EQU8, FACEIT and others), detected locally only by files and folders in the install folder (at most 4 levels, 50,000 names, 2 seconds, never into links) and by the game's processes. When the check is not possible, it does not freeze either. Once per game session a message says why.
- The same rule holds for the parental pause when time is up: a game with anti-cheat keeps running, CouchHUD stays in front of it, the reminder and the close after the grace time stay the same.

**Settings tidied up: HUD as the first page**
- The first category shows only what matters most: the setup assistant at the top (before hidden under Info), then one "Mode" row (App or Shell), the couch lock with its code, the way to parental controls and one compact status line (startup, automatic sign-in).
- "Mode" leads to the guided shell mode setup or the way back to normal Windows; in Expert mode also "Only set the mode". The double ways are gone: the integration level, the couch lock and the code are no longer also under Windows integration, the lock, startup and mode values no longer repeated as facts below. Parental controls link to the one place.
- Every gate stays exactly as strict: the lock and the code only with the code (with parental controls on, the parents' code), setup and the way back only unlocked, confirmations unchanged, Windows integration keeps the shell, sign-in and emergency options in Expert mode only.
- New names: the two modes are now called **App** (formerly "Light", a normal full-screen app) and **Shell** (formerly "Full", "full integration" or "Console", CouchHUD starts instead of the desktop), everywhere you read them: the assistant, Settings, "What CouchHUD changes in Windows", notices, the installer, the guide and the README. The button is now "Set up shell mode" (formerly "Set up CouchHUD as a console"), along with "Check and repair shell mode" and "Finish shell mode setup". The first settings category is now called **HUD** instead of "Console", and every path ("Settings › HUD › Mode") follows. Stored values stay the same (`integrationMode` is still `light`/`full`), nothing changes on existing installs.
- Assistant, "Areas" step: the list fits between the subtitle and the hint again instead of covering both (wider, flatter rows).

**Documentation in English**
- Documentation in English: the README has a full English part (switch at the very top), the English guide (`docs/GUIDE.en.md`) now matches the German one section by section, and there are new `CONTRIBUTING.en.md`, `docs/SKINS.en.md`, `docs/PRIVACY.en.md` and `docs/DISCLAIMER.en.md`. Every file links to its other language at the top; for the disclaimer and the privacy notice the German version prevails in case of doubt.

**Skins renamed**
- The built-in skins no longer carry third-party brand names: "PS4 Classic" is now **Deep Sea**, "Xbox" **Greenlight**, "Cyberpunk" **Neon City**; CouchHUD and Nova stay. Names and descriptions follow the language setting (Tiefsee, Grünlicht, Neonstadt in German), and the sound sets are named like their skins. The look stays, with own colors instead of brand colors: Greenlight in a teal green with a diamond as mouse pointer and a bar instead of a ring when a game starts, Neon City in an own lemon yellow; Deep Sea no longer brings its own button symbols.
- A chosen skin or sound set with the old name (household and every profile) is switched to the new one once when the settings are read; unknown names still fall back to the default skin. Your own skins (`user:<folder>`) are not affected.
- An older CouchHUD does not know the new names and shows the default skin after the first save with the new names.

**Startup options for found games**
- Startup parameters and a working folder are no longer only for own entries but for every found game that starts an exe (game folders, GOG, plugins). Stored as an override, a rescan keeps them, "Reset" starts it again as found.
- Own start target: "Start this file instead" takes an `.exe`, `.bat` or `.cmd` in the game's folder, picked in the file browser, for every source with an install folder (Steam, Epic, Ubisoft too). Play time, detection of the running game and parental controls keep counting for the same entry.
- Honest per source: Steam games say that their launch options are set in Steam (Library › game › Properties), Epic and Ubisoft that the launcher takes no parameters; nothing is pretended, nothing is written into Steam's files.
- Edit › Startup options (expert mode only). The detail page shows "Own startup options" when some are active.
- Security: changing them needs the unlocked couch, with parental controls the parents' code for exactly this entry (apps too), not while a profile waits, not through the phone remote, MCP, plugins or the assistant (contract tests). The path is stored canonically and checked again at every start (local, no share, no device name, no link, inside the game's folder). Batch files start through `System32\cmd.exe /d /v:off /s /c` without the shell; parameters with `& | ^ < > ( ) % !` or quotes inside a value are refused, now also for own entries with a batch file. Details in SECURITY.md.
- Security, tightened: a batch file as the start target counts only with the content that was approved (size and SHA-256, compared on the handle kept open); changed, it does not start, and parents confirm it by picking it again. Batch files without a kept content (older overrides, own and found entries) start only on an open couch. Folder links below the game's folder are caught when saving and at every start, a batch file's working folder must lie inside the game's folder, its parameters are plain characters only. The parents' code is used up only once the change or the start went through; the client and the foreground follow the target really started.

**Store match and own info**
- Edit › Store entry: search the Steam store and pick the right entry from a list of hits; description, age rating and pictures then come from there. "No store entry" when none fits, "Automatic again" resets it. Steam games keep their own entry.
- Edit › Info: override description, developer, publisher, release date and genres with your own text, on the on-screen keyboard, each field back to the store's on its own.
- The edit menu is reordered: name, artwork, info, store entry, startup options (expert mode only), game/app, hide. Artwork moved from the detail page into the edit menu.
- The automatic match errs less often: none rather than a wrong one. Roman numerals as characters (Ⅶ), full-width letters and ß are understood; names in another script, different years in brackets and ambiguous hits no longer give a match. It goes by the name the entry was found with; renaming no longer changes it.
- When a game later loses its known rating in the automatic match or gets a milder one, the rating so far stays as a floor until parents confirm.
- Security: with parental controls every change of the match needs the parents' code for exactly this entry; without it the stricter rating of the old and the new match counts until the parents confirm. Own texts never change the rating, are bounded and shown as text. Not through the phone remote, MCP, plugins or the assistant (contract tests); the search uses the existing store endpoint with size and time limits. Details in SECURITY.md.

**Profiles**
- Several profiles on one couch, up to 8: every profile has its own favorites, collections, recently played games, its own play time account and its own rules. Design/color, language, sounds/music and animations apply per profile; the switches stay where they always were and act on the active profile. Everything else stays a household setting; a daily limit exists only per profile.
- Full-screen profile picker "Who is playing?" (picture, name, color, a lock for its own code), at the start only when there is more than one profile. Switching also from the guide menu ("Switch profile").
- Settings › Parental controls › Profiles, entirely with the controller: create (child or adult profile), rename, picture and color from presets, rules per profile, an own code for child profiles, delete. Every change needs the parents' code for exactly that change.
- A child profile can have its own code (stored only as a salted hash, with its own wrong-code brake); it opens only its own profile. Without a code of its own, siblings can use the profile (a hint in management). In place of a child's code only the parents' code counts, never the unlocked couch; a child's code, the parents' code and the couch code cannot be the same. Every profile that is not a child profile is reached only with the parents' code once parental controls are on and there are several profiles; a default profile with rules counts as a child profile (existing installs). A profile whose time is up or that is blocked is not left at all without the parents' code. No switch while a game runs.
- An adult profile really has no limits, a child profile takes over the default profile's rules; a profile that changes kind gets the matching rules.
- After the screensaver, the display going off and a restart the profile picker is in front again when the active profile was protected, also after 10 minutes without real input (fixed, no setting), never in the middle of a game. With parental controls and several profiles only the parents change the screensaver and display off. A new child profile leads straight to its rules, with a hint when it has no limits yet. While it waits, the host takes only the picker, code entry, volume, power and display; the guide menu, resuming, launches, the assistant and MCP stay closed.
- On a switch: first parents' grants, the couch browser, recent sites and the couch unlock are dropped, then the switch (the couch is locked afterwards), the old play time account is written, the parental controls are rebuilt for the new profile, library, favorites, collections and language reloaded, every parents' grant and revealed recent site dropped, an open couch browser closed. A deleted profile's play time account stays renamed (`….deleted-<time>`) as a record.
- Security: the phone remote, MCP, the assistant and plugins cannot switch, create anything or set codes; while the profile picker or management is open, keys and text from the phone do not count (contract tests). Enforcement in the service is planned, today the host checks in the user's account; threat model and limits in SECURITY.md.

**CaseHUD: choose and operate, set up on the PC**
- The CaseHUD page chooses and operates: displays on/off, scene, scene switching, next scene, set for all, AWTRIX, identify; per display brightness, theme (light/dark) and mode; lighting on/off, brightness and an effect from CaseHUD's list (CaseHUD remembers the parameters per effect). Everything that builds is gone: the design grid per display, rotation/mounting, community import, effect parameters (colours, speed …) and devices on/off, with their bridge methods (`caseHudDesigns`, `caseHudCommunity`, `caseHudImport`), client code, texts and tests. In their place one line says "You set effects, devices and designs in CaseHUD on the PC", and A opens CaseHUD's window.
- New: **automation on/off** (`PATCH /automation`; the rules only in CaseHUD's window), needing the unlock like scene switching.
- New: a short warning when CaseHUD reports programs that may block its devices (`conflicts`, OpenRGB say), pointing to CaseHUD on the PC.
- The scene shown comes from `status.scene` (CaseHUD 1.0); with older versions from the active scene of the list, last from the rotation.
- Texts: the scene conflict (409) now names the real reason, an automation rule holding the displays. "Not authorised", "refused" and "not found" (also a deleted scene) get our own German and English texts; CaseHUD's English message only goes to the log.
- Security: the host takes only brightness, theme and mode for a display, only on/off, brightness and a listed effect for the lighting, and refuses everything else. Names from CaseHUD (conflicts, scene, scenes, displays, sets, effects) are cleaned (no control or bidi characters), capped and shown as text only; the ids that address things stay unchanged. "Set for all" takes only a set CaseHUD lists under `/sets`, in the light or dark theme, and only known displays (at most 64). A refused token while changing a display or the lighting makes the host read the key again, as for every other call.

**VPN** (Plan for 0.5, item 1)
- **NordVPN from the couch**: new page Settings › Network › VPN and two rows in the Guide menu (connect or disconnect with the country chosen last, choose a country). State connected, connecting, not connected, with the country or server chosen here and the tunnel type (NordLynx/OpenVPN); quick connect, a country from a fixed list, a specific server in expert mode, *Open NordVPN on the PC* to sign in. No VPN client of its own, no credentials: CouchHUD runs the installed NordVPN app's command line (`-c`, `-c -g`, `-c -n`, `-d`, as NordVPN's help center documents) and reads the state off the VPN adapter and the routing table (Meshnet alone does not count as connected), without asking third parties. A country whose change the tunnel did not visibly go through shows as "requested". Hints for not installed, not in Program Files and "no connection, sign in to the NordVPN app". New expert setting *VPN in the Guide menu* (on by default).
- Security: `NordVPN.exe` only from the HKLM entry or Program Files with a canonical path and no reparse points (code shared with CaseHUD's interface switch), countries and servers only from a fixed list or a strict pattern, `ArgumentList`, a time limit, one command line at a time. Reading works on a locked couch, every change needs the unlock, with parental controls the parents' code. Provider interface `IVpnProvider` for more VPNs; deliberately no WireGuard profiles (they would need administrator rights).

**Smart home**
- **Choose devices**: the Smart home tab no longer lists every Home Assistant entity (well over a thousand in big homes), only the chosen ones, in the chosen order. With nothing chosen (new, or after the update) it offers the picker. The picker groups by Home Assistant areas (by type otherwise), with type filters, search on the on-screen keyboard, suggestions (lights, switches, climate, blinds, media players, scenes and scripts, fans, locks; not hidden ones) and controller reordering. At most 500 entries, stored in `homeAssistantEntities`. Areas, hidden entities and categories (device settings, diagnostics) come from Home Assistant's WebSocket API (no admin token needed, only a token Home Assistant already accepted, a refused one never again); without them the picker groups by type. Entries pinned to the guide menu so far are the starting choice. Chosen entries that are not reachable right now can be dropped. Answers from Home Assistant are limited in size and time (15 seconds); CouchHUD follows no redirects but shows the target address to enter instead.
- Switching, the Guide menu and the AI assistant/MCP only reach chosen or pinned entities. The couch lock still allows only lights, scenes, media players and pinned switches; the choice itself needs the unlock. Only chosen entries can be pinned to the Guide menu; unchoosing one also unpins it.
- **Sign in with Home Assistant** (plan for 0.5, item 7): no token to type any more. The button (Settings › Weather & smart home, simple mode too) asks for the address and opens Home Assistant's sign-in page in the built-in browser (Home Assistant's OAuth flow, `/auth/authorize` and `/auth/token`). CouchHUD catches the redirect with the code without loading it, exchanges the code at the same Home Assistant and keeps the refresh token encrypted with DPAPI like the token so far; the access token lives in memory only and is renewed automatically. *Sign out* revokes the access in Home Assistant (`/auth/revoke`, older versions `action=revoke`). The long-lived token stays as the expert way (expert mode only); a token replaces the sign-in and the other way round.
- Security: `state` with 256 random bits, single use, 10 minutes; the redirect only to `https://couchhud.invalid/auth/callback` (never resolves, never loaded, no open port) and only from the Home Assistant page of that sign-in; the exchange only at the address the sign-in started with, through the same client as before (TLS check, no redirects, size and time limits). Code and state never in the address bar, the history, the recent sites or the log. Signing in and out need the unlock like setting the token; the sign-in page extends no unlock (only real input counts). Threat model in SECURITY.md. Still to be tested on the TV/in the VM with a real Home Assistant.
- Sign-in tightened: PKCE (S256) on every sign-in, the verifier in memory only next to the state (older Home Assistant versions ignore it). When Home Assistant refuses the refresh token (`invalid_grant`), no further request goes out (failed logins could get the PC banned by Home Assistant's IP ban); the settings offer *sign in again*. `invalid_request` on the code exchange also means "sign in again". The current connection stays until the new sign-in is saved; a sign-in that finishes after a sign-out, a change of address or token, or that cannot be saved is revoked in Home Assistant at once. After a renewal an access token goes only to the address of its sign-in, never to one changed meanwhile. Every answer of the sign-in (code, refusal, wrong page, too late) closes the browser with the sign-in page; requests to `couchhud.invalid` are answered by the browser itself with 403. When the sign-in comes from another page, the hint points to the final address (e.g. https instead of http).
- Sign-in, last details: the state is checked before the expiry, so a foreign page cannot end an expired sign-in and close the browser. When opening the sign-in page fails, its special status for the address bar and the recent sites ends too. The 403 filter for `couchhud.invalid` also covers shared and service workers where the WebView2 runtime can. A sign-in to another server resets the entity choice and the pinned entries (the old server's ids do not fit).

**Retest of taking over the 0.4.0 lock**
- "Back to normal Windows" and the setup show no step status until the couch is open ("Not checked yet"); while the 0.4.0 lock waits for an administrator's confirmation, the page says so. Cancelling goes back. Long notices before a UAC prompt appear low on the screen, so the page title stays visible.
- Setup: when another program holds the CouchHUD service's connection (named pipe), the message says exactly that and advises a restart and running the setup again. Exit code 20 and fail-closed stay.
- Uninstall: empty task folders `\CouchHud\<SID>` and `\CouchHud` are removed (only empty ones, only below `\CouchHud`); registry keys stay.
- Tests: the update tests' folders live under a short temp path (MAX_PATH with a long repository path).

**Downloads and games not installed**
- **Downloads tab** (also in the Guide menu, badge "⬇ 2 running · 45 %" at the top right): everything that downloads or installs, in one place: Steam installs and updates with progress, Windows updates, app updates (winget), CouchHUD's own update, plugins and skins from the catalog, installing and updating CaseHUD, and game installs you started. Bars only where the source really knows the progress; Epic shows "Running in the client" without a percentage. GOG, EA app, Ubisoft Connect, Battle.net and Microsoft Store don't show up because they don't store their progress readably. A notification when a game has finished installing or updating; it is in the library then. Finished entries stay for a day (20 at most). Replaces the former Steam download list in the Guide menu.
- **Games not installed** (Downloads tab, or X in the Games tab): Steam and Epic games you own but haven't installed here, as a paged cover grid with store filter and search. A asks and opens the client's install dialog through its own link (`steam://install/<AppID>`, `com.epicgames.launcher://apps/<namespace>%3A<catalog id>%3A<AppName>?action=install`), never anything else; the ids are strictly checked. Only local files are read: for Steam the games the signed-in account played before (`userdata\<account>\config\localconfig.vdf`) with name and type from `appcache\appinfo.vdf` (formats 39 to 41), for Epic the launcher's catalog cache. No account data, no login, no new network access except covers through the existing artwork cache. A locked couch has to be unlocked first; with parental controls the parents' code for exactly that game. Parsers with size, depth and time limits.
- **Size of Windows updates:** tiny updates (Defender definitions, cumulative updates) were listed at over 2 GB, because CouchHUD showed `MaxDownloadSize`, the full package with every variant; Windows usually loads only an express or delta part. Now a size is shown only when the smallest and the largest download are at most a factor of 1.5 apart; otherwise there is no size (an "up to 2 GB" still reads as 2 GB on a TV). Updates that are already downloaded say "Already downloaded" instead of a size. The total appears only when every update still to load has a sure size, never as the sum of a part. While downloading, the Downloads tab shows the Windows Update Agent's real counters (`IDownloadProgress`: loaded of total), reported through the service; they go once the installation starts. Values from the agent count as foreign data: ≤ 0, NaN, text and more than 1 TiB count as unknown.
- **Downloads tab: start and control what really works.** Pending updates are startable entries in the tab: Windows updates (all, as on the Updates page), app updates (winget), CouchHUD's update and newer plugins and skins; A starts them through the same functions and bridge commands as the Updates page, with the same questions. A on a running entry offers only what really works for its source: CouchHUD's update can be cancelled while it downloads (no pause: a cancelled setup is downloaded and verified again in full on the next start, the partial file is deleted). An accepted cancel still stops the update when the setup is already fully downloaded and verified; from the hand-over to the service on it is refused instead of confirmed. An update that has just finished is not offered again until the Updates page knows the new state. Steam entries open Steam's download page (`steam://open/downloads`), where pausing and resuming work; Steam has no documented way to pause from outside. Windows Update and winget run to the end: there is no cancel through the service yet, and a cancelled installer can leave an app broken. Epic has no fixed link to its downloads, there "Stop tracking" stays. On a locked couch and for a waiting profile all of it is closed, Steam's download page needs the parents' code with parental controls on; the remote, MCP, the assistant and plugins don't reach the new commands (contract tests).

**TV**
- **TV control** (Settings › TV, plan for 0.5, item 3): pair LG TVs with webOS over their network interface (search in the home network via SSDP, or type the address in expert mode; the TV asks once for permission), then switch on and off, volume (◀▶) and mute, choose the input. In the Guide menu: TV on/off, TV volume and TV input (can be turned off). Switching on uses Wake-on-LAN to the paired TV's MAC address (read from the TV while pairing, otherwise from Windows' ARP cache, or entered in expert mode); "Turn on via Wi-Fi" or "Mobile TV On" must be on in the TV's settings.
- Expert mode, off by default: *Turn on the TV with CouchHUD* (when CouchHUD starts and when the PC wakes up) and *Turn off the TV with the PC* (sleep and shutdown from CouchHUD, Windows sleep where possible; only while the TV shows the app or input the PC used while pairing).
- Security: only home-network addresses (private or link-local), encrypted connection (wss, port 3001) with the certificate pinned at pairing (if the TV shows a different one, the page says "Pair TV again" and CouchHUD sends nothing); unencrypted (port 3000) only for older TVs without wss, only in expert mode after a question, never for a TV (address or MAC) that was paired with encryption before, and shown as such; pairing again with a different certificate says so clearly. The pairing key is stored DPAPI-encrypted in the config, never in the log or the UI. While the couch is locked, on/off, volume and mute work (checked in the host too); pairing, changing the TV and switching inputs need the unlock, with parental controls also the parent code for exactly the chosen input. The phone remote and MCP/assistant do not control the TV. No cloud, no telemetry. HDMI-CEC and other brands are prepared but not implemented yet.

**Browser**
- The browser stopped reacting to the controller after browsing to a page: the page's dialogs (alert, confirm, prompt, "Leave site?"), sign-in windows, the color picker and the Windows touch keyboard opened windows of their own, CouchHUD lost the foreground and dropped every button afterwards. Now a page only asks in the HUD (with the site's name, answered with the controller, "no" after 2 minutes, dialog loops are stopped), "Leave site?" is no longer asked, basic auth and certificate errors are refused, and B and the guide button close a popup of the page (a select list).
- The controller on the page follows one fixed rule: the left stick is only the cursor (it no longer throws the controller into the bar or scrolls on the side, TV pages no longer get double steps), d-pad = arrow keys, RT = Enter, LT = Tab, X = text into the page's selected field through the HUD keyboard (password fields masked, without the phone), guide always leads out, B always back. Keys go straight into the page instead of to whichever window has the keyboard focus.
- The HUD and the host always agree on who has the controller (the host answers every request and reports its own changes); a watchdog checks the state twice a second, restarts the cursor or hands the controller back to the HUD, and reloads a hung page after about 15 seconds (if it hangs again, a "page hangs" page shows); crashed pages are reloaded, a crashed browser is closed.
- Smoother: the cursor has its own clock (8 ms instead of 16–31 ms), no Windows pointer acceleration, and stays on the page, also after a change of monitor or scale; the page state reaches the HUD at most once per round, recent sites are stored only when they change; no screensaver while the page plays sound or a full-screen video (at most 3 hours without input); input on the page counts for the screensaver like input in the HUD.
- An own error page for unreachable sites (without scripts; not for downloads, 204 or the server's own error pages); "Reload" tries the address again. Address field: "1.10" or "3.5" are a search, `localhost:3000`, IP addresses and `[::1]` open.
- Downloads only after a question at the TV (not from the phone, "no" by default, file type shown on its own) and only into the Downloads folder, never over an existing file, with a cleaned file name; programs, scripts, shortcuts, installers and disk images (.exe, .msi, .bat, .ps1, .lnk, .url, .scf, .iso …) are never downloaded; controller clicks only land on the page itself; text from X only goes into the page and field X was pressed on; page questions are visibly marked as the web's; WebAuthn, share, eye dropper and payment windows blocked; permissions (camera, microphone, location, notifications …) are always denied; app links (mailto:, steam: …) and other schemes are blocked with a note; password and form autofill off.
- No page clicks without input: the watchdog and CouchHUD's own scripts run without a user gesture (before, a page could open windows or go full screen without a click). A page goes full screen only right after real input, otherwise it is refused; in full screen the HUD keeps a "Website in full screen" strip at the top that no page can paint over.
- Several dialogs in a row (alert, then confirm) are all asked; dialog loops are stopped even when each dialog waits for its answer. Download and 204 links leave the page in place instead of an error page. After a mouse click beside the page the focus is visibly on the bar, Enter brings the page back, F6 also switches in the HUD.
- Certificate errors and required sign-ins get their own error page, a hanging page the title "Page hangs". Network and file paths (`\\server\share`, `C:\…`) are refused instead of searched, searches never start with `!` or `\`. More blocked download types (themes, remote desktop, certificates, updates, Access); renamed downloads (.download, .crdownload) are checked by their real name.
- Reading with the stick alone keeps the screensaver away; the wheel scrolls only on the page.
- No picture-in-picture (Document PiP neither): it would float above the HUD and the full-screen strip. After the guide button or leaving, no page question shows over the HUD; the strip shows the new site after a redirect, and the manual full screen ends when another site loads. More blocked download types (sandbox, web queries, web archives).
- The warning "The browser is about to close" (30 seconds before the 2-minute unlock runs out) could not be seen with the page in full screen, the page covered the HUD's notice. It is now shown with a countdown in the full-screen strip at the top, which no page can paint over, cover or imitate. Any key or controller button extends as before, then the warning is gone. The warning itself makes no input; what the page does still does not extend. A long host name is shortened in the middle (at most 48 characters, the end of the domain stays); the web mark and the warning always stay fully visible. A screen reader announces the start of the warning once, not every second of the countdown.
- The recently visited sites on the browser start page now stay hidden with parental controls on, also at the unlocked couch. "Shown with the parents’ code" stands in their place; pressing it asks for a fresh entry of the parents’ code (given for exactly this, used once) and shows them for this session, until the couch is locked again, a game starts, one comes back to the HUD from a game or parental controls are switched. This covers "an adult unlocked the couch and walked away". It does not keep out a child who knows the code: with parental controls, the couch code and the parents’ code are the same code. Fail-closed: without a clear answer from the host they stay hidden; every change of the lock or of parental controls sends a new state to the start page.
- Single recent sites can be removed with Y on the browser's start page (after a question). Only while they are visible: not at the locked couch, and with parental controls only after the parents' code.
- The "Games" and "Games not installed" pages and the artwork picker (cover, hero, logo) did not scroll down because they lacked the grid layout rule (for "Games" since the page was renamed for the switchable areas).
- New setting "Remember recently visited sites" (Settings › Library, part of the Browser area). Off: the built-in browser keeps no history and deletes the sites already remembered.

**Power menu by mode**
- In shell mode there are "PC mode" (back with the Xbox button) and "Restart into PC mode". In app mode "Quit CouchHUD" takes that place, and there is no restart into PC mode, since Windows starts with the desktop anyway. Applies to the Start menu, the guide menu and the settings.

**Look**
- No shadow under logos any more, and none under covers at rest. The glow around the chosen cover has enough room and is no longer cut off at the edge of the row; rows and grids fade out softly at their edges.
- The top bar never runs into the status on the right: tabs are a little narrower, clearly so in Neon City; if it is still too tight, the tabs fade out and the active tab stays in view.
- Unread notifications come right after the running game in the guide menu.
- Lists and menus (settings, guide menu, dialogs) mark the chosen row with a frame only, without a glow that got cut off at the edge; in every skin, Stardust and HDR included.
- Areas (Settings › HUD) apply when you leave the dialog with B, no extra "Apply".

**Continue playing and save games** (Plan for 0.5, item 4)
- **"Continue playing" on Home:** The shelf starts with the games played last (at most 6, the running game first), the first card big with the wide picture. Under the title: "last played" as a relative time, the total play time and the last session (now stored when a game ends; a game then also counts as played last when the launcher does not record it). Above the shelf it says "Continue playing" while the focus is there. Hidden entries and apps stay out, locked games appear as in the library, the start checks as before. Progress (achievements) deliberately not: Steam's local stats files are binary, depend on the game's schema and are not reliably current.
- **Automatic save backup after playing:** When a game ends (started or detected by CouchHUD), CouchHUD backs up its known save folders 20 s later in the background with low priority. Unchanged saves are not backed up again. Limits: backups per game (default 3, apart from your own), size per game (1 GB), space for all backups (10 GB), 5000 files, 10 minutes. There is a notification only on errors or reached limits. Settings › Library: *Back up saves after playing* (on/off) in simple mode too, the limits, folder and retention in expert mode. Automatic backups are marked in the backup picker.
- Security: Restore and delete need an unlocked couch, with parental controls the parents' code for exactly this game (host and UI). When backing up, files behind junctions/links and in other profiles are skipped, a backup folder behind a link or on a network share is refused. When restoring, CouchHUD refuses link and folder entries in the ZIP and duplicate targets, checks every target again right before writing (no link, no foreign profile), writes at most the stated length and requires enough free space. Everything runs as the user in the CouchHUD process, never through the service. Threat model in SECURITY.md.
- After the security review: only a reparse point that really redirects (junction, symbolic link, mount point) counts as a link; OneDrive and other cloud files are backed up and restored normally, only files held online are left out by the automatic backup (no download). With parental controls the backup settings need the parents' code too; the size limit per game also holds for backups by hand; a backup folder in the save places is refused and never backed up along. Also checked: the final path of opened files, the backup folder's whole chain from the drive, device names and names ending in a dot or space; files with several hard links stay out. Restoring writes to a new temp file with a random name, accepts at most 20,000 entries and removes folders it created on failure; the rotation deletes only readable backups; automatic backups run one after the other.
- After the second review: when files are held online only, the automatic backup waits (nothing written, nothing rotated, a log entry, a quiet hint at most once a day per game). Short 8.3 names are resolved when comparing the final path, a volume without a drive letter stays closed. The temp file when restoring has a short name. A profile moved with a junction gets a clear message about the backup folder. A backup by hand of an unchanged state makes no new version and says "unchanged".
- After the third review: "unchanged" now means exactly the files in the manifest of the last backup by hand (paths, size, change time per file); a restored older state or one copied in with old time stamps is backed up anew, an automatic backup is no reference, and before uninstalling a new backup is always made. `OFFLINE` alone no longer stops the automatic backup (only with a cloud tag or the RECALL attributes). Backup folders are recognised through 8.3 names too, and a restore never writes into the backup folder.
- After the fourth review: before uninstalling "unchanged" holds again when a backup by hand has exactly these files, so backing up and cancelling over and over rotates no older states away. Incomplete backups by hand have a limit of their own (as many as are kept by hand). When restoring, the real path of every opened temp file is compared with the backup folder's real path (also through `subst` drives); if a path or 8.3 name cannot be resolved, it is refused.

**Battery levels**
- Laptops and handhelds: the PC's battery sits next to the clock at the top (percent, ⚡ on the charger, yellow from 15%). A PC without a battery shows nothing. At 15% "Battery low" comes once, again only after charging to 20% or on the charger.
- Controllers: the guide menu shows every controller with four steps (XInput reports steps, not percent; wired 🔌) and every connected Bluetooth device that tells Windows its battery (controllers, headphones) in percent. The Bluetooth list shows it next to the name. When a controller runs low, a small 🎮 with bars shows at the top, and "Battery low" comes once per controller.
- No new setting. Read-only queries only (GetSystemPowerStatus, XInputGetBatteryInformation, the device property DEVPKEY_Bluetooth_Battery), once a minute and when the charger is plugged in or out, never on the UI thread; nothing through the service, the remote, MCP or plugins.

**Small fixes**
- VM test, store match: after picking a store entry the detail page kept showing "No description" until it was opened again. Causes: a store fetch still running, slow (1.5 s gap, Retry-After) or failed came back as "no description" and was not cached; an older answer could overwrite the newer one on the page; only the top page was refreshed; a waiting fetch used the match from before it waited; and a cache entry from older versions also stood for a picked entry. Now the host says whether the store has answered yet (`storePending`); the page shows "loading" and asks again after 2, 5 and 10 s, only the newest answer counts, every open detail page of the entry reloads, the match is read only behind the lock, and an old cache entry only counts for the automatic match.
- Sizes: decimal everywhere (1 MB = 1,000,000 bytes, one shared formatter in the page and the host): downloads, updates, library sizes, save games, image cache. Before, MiB were shown as "MB". The automatic save backup limits are now decimal MB (default 1000 per game, 10,000 in total). Limits stored by older versions (binary MB) are moved up once when first read to the next offered limit above them (1024 → 2000, 10,240 → 20,000; marker `saveLimitsDecimal`), so no game that was backed up before is refused now; the picker shows them as chosen. Memory in the quick info stays as in Task Manager.
- Batch file as start target: runs without a console window in the taskbar (`CREATE_NO_WINDOW`), otherwise unchanged (held until it ends, checksum, working folder, parameters, play time). A hidden prompt (`pause`, `set /p`, `choice`) could not be ended from CouchHUD; so the launcher ends exactly its own cmd.exe (process id and start time, never by name) when 3 checks in a row (about 15 s) find no working child process, from 30 s of run time on or earlier once its game session is over, with a notice "Start script … was waiting for input – ended"; when CouchHUD quits, every one without a working child. A game started in the foreground and waiting loops with `timeout` or `tasklist` keep the script alive.
- Detail page: the effective age rating shows as a small badge ("18+") above developer and release date, also a kept higher rating; a floor above 21 shows as "18+", unknown shows nothing. Display only.
- Browser: at the locked couch the start page no longer shows the recently visited sites (no titles, no addresses), only a row "Recently visited sites" saying that the code shows them. The host reports the lock state with the browser state and on every change; with no answer or an unknown state the list stays hidden.
- Save games: an incomplete backup by hand with exactly the files of the last incomplete one (paths, size, change time) is no longer kept: the older one holds, nothing is rotated away. Pressing "Back up now" again and again while one file cannot be read no longer pushes older states out. The message says "unchanged, still incomplete"; before an uninstall CouchHUD still asks. The same holds for the automatic backup after playing, there without a notice.
- Setup: test builds with a suffix (`/DAppVersion=0.5.0-test`) compile; the version resource gets only the numbers, AppVersion and the file name the full text.
- The development state on `master` carries the version `0.5.0-dev`.
- Tests: the restricted-token test (`PipeTests`) switches identity itself and runs only the prepared CreateFileW call as the token; before, managed code of `RunImpersonated` ran under the token and, depending on the test order, sometimes tried to load an assembly and failed.
- Browser: the recently visited sites keep only origin and path, never a query, fragment or user name/password in the address (they can hold tokens, codes and search terms). A page that rewrites its address with `history.pushState` no longer fills the list: one entry per origin and path, a new visit replaces the old one, at most 6. Lists stored by older versions are cleaned to this rule at start and written back without queries.
- Log: the WPF control of WebView2 writes its background colour into CouchHUD's environment as `WEBVIEW2_DEFAULT_BACKGROUND_COLOR`; the browser removes it before every start like any `WEBVIEW2_*` variable and logged that every time. Now this one variable is logged once per session; any other `WEBVIEW2_*` variable is still logged every time.
- Tests: the test "service stops right after the match" (`ReviewFixTests`) failed now and then. The cause was the test pipe server: it reported ready before it waited for connections; when the client (which must send nothing) dropped before that, the wait ended with "the pipe is being closed" and the server with an error. The server now waits before it reports ready and copes with a connection dropped early; instead of a fixed 100 or 200 ms the tests wait until the server is done with the connection and only then check that nothing arrived. 200 repetitions without a failure.
- Parental controls: the remaining time is never reported negative any more. A game that runs on in the grace period before it is closed keeps counting (as it should); the status then said e.g. `remainingSeconds: -49`, and `expired` was false as soon as no game ran, because it only described the running title. Now the remaining time stays at 0 and `expired` is true once the time is used up or the window is closed, also with no game running and before the next tick; an app that does not count is not affected. This never gave more time: the start check (no start under 60 s) and the expiry (at 0 s) already treated the negative value as used up. Extra time from the parents is still taken from the overdrawn account.
- Browser: a very long host no longer breaks over several lines in "Recently visited sites" and the bookmarks. It is cut to 48 characters like in the full-screen strip (start and end of the domain stay), title and host end in "…" on their line.
- Development: `scripts/deploy-vm.ps1 -Service` also updates the service in the test VM: publishes it like the release build, stops it, replaces `<InstallDir>\service\CouchHud.Service.exe` (data folder and access list stay, owner Administrators as the setup sets it) and starts it again, also when the copy fails. `scripts/vm-cdp.ps1 -Disable` deletes the key `HKLM\SOFTWARE\CouchHud` when it is empty afterwards.
- Developer switches: when `HKLM\SOFTWARE\CouchHud` holds values (`DeveloperMode`, `RemoteDebuggingPort`) but non-administrators can change the key, the start writes exactly one warning line to the log (names only, no values). Until now a remote debugging port set this way was dropped silently without `--dev`.
- Code entry: a notice no longer covers the lock symbol. Toasts now take up to 60rem of width instead of half the screen (a longer text wrapped into a tall block, above all at 1280 wide), and on the code entry they show low, above the hints.
- Settings: after a hard reset `config.json` held only zero bytes (the temp file was swapped in without being written through, only the rename was on disk). Saving now goes through a temp file in the same folder, written through (WriteThrough and flush) and then swapped; the state before stays as `config.json.bak`. If `config.json` is empty, full of zero bytes, only `null` or broken, CouchHUD takes a valid `.bak`, puts the broken file aside unchanged as `config.json.broken-<time>` and says so in the log and the HUD. Without a usable `.bak` it runs closed as before (locked, parental controls on, nothing saved); a file holding only `null` used to become defaults without a lock, that is fixed. Play time accounts and the wrong-code brakes of children's codes are written the same way (with `.bak`), the list of approved plugins is written through, on purpose without `.bak`. An unreadable wrong-code brake no longer gives fresh tries. The service does not take over the stand-in of a broken `config.json` as its lock mode.
- Profiles: when CouchHUD falls back to the profile picker (10 minutes without input, screensaver, display off), the couch unlock is dropped as well. The settings (with the MCP key) can no longer be fetched without a chosen profile, the MCP key is no longer sent while the couch is locked, and the parental status is reduced to what is needed until a profile is chosen.
- Profiles: an MCP launch (and any other request with a confirmation) is refused at once while the profile picker waits, instead of waiting until the timeout for a confirmation that can never show.
- Profiles: the waiting profile picker is neutral: the household's language and skin (the default profile), no remaining time of the last active profile. The picker deliberately shows no remaining time per profile, since anyone at the couch sees it; after the switch it shows in the HUD as usual.
- Profiles: a name over 24 characters is refused instead of cut silently. The default profile without a name of its own is called "Default" in the picker, the management and the menu title. Deleting a profile renames the `.bak` of its play time account to `….deleted-<time>` as well.
- Log: a refused profile call (e.g. wrong code, a game running) is one line in the log instead of a full stack trace.
- Settings, after the review: the `config.json.bak` gets the new state right after every save as well; before, a reset in the window of the swap could bring back the state before parental controls were switched on. A valid temp file left behind that is newer than the `.bak` wins when restoring. The "restored" notice rests on a marker file `config.json.restored` (time, source, name of the broken copy) and is no longer lost when the watcher or the sign-in lock loads the file first. The file is read again before it is put aside; if the other process repaired it meanwhile, it stays. Broken copies (`.broken-*` and old `*.bad`) are kept to 5, old temp files are cleaned up at start. A play time account holding only `null` counts as broken (copy or tampering), not as an empty account. The uninstall (as an administrator) reads `config.json` only through `ConfigStore.Peek` (no restore, cleanup or writing in the user's folder, no links); a contract test keeps the service, the privileged helpers, the uninstall and the elevated release of the lock policies free of the writing helpers. The "restored" notice goes only after it was shown.
- Settings, VM test with a hard reset right after saving: the content of every temp file was on the disk, the last rename was not (NTFS writes its log lazily). Afterwards `config.json` was new and the `.bak` old, and the next start deleted the complete newer temp file as a leftover; in the same way `config.json` could have been valid but old, losing a parental rule made stricter just before. Now the folder is flushed with FlushFileBuffers after every swap (that writes the NTFS log with the rename), for `config.json`, play time accounts and wrong-code brakes. Independently, a leftover temp file that is valid, newer than `config.json` or the `.bak` and written before the current Windows start is taken at start instead of deleted (one from this start may belong to a save of the other CouchHUD process running right now, one dated in the future never wins); right before the swap it is checked that nobody saved since, and the temp file goes afterwards; a cut-off (invalid) or older one goes as before. When a reader holds the `.bak` open, the save still lands, only the `.bak` keeps its older state.
- Settings: the notice "Settings cannot be used" promised "no game starts" also where the lock and parental controls are only advice (not the shell, PC mode); it now says what applies in each mode. Saving in this state no longer reports "locked or belongs to another program" but that nothing is written into the damaged file on purpose.
- Code entry: medium and long messages covered the keyboard legend below the keys at 1920 and 1280 wide. The code entry keeps room for the message at the bottom, the lock and the legend stay visible.
- Log: warnings and errors (start, restore, the locked stand-in) now reach the disk as well, at most once a second; before, the last lines of a start were missing after a hard reset. Ordinary lines follow together, at the latest after 5 seconds; the flush to the disk no longer holds up another thread that is logging, and closing the log does a flush still due. Zero bytes a hard reset leaves at the end of `couchhud.log` are removed by the next start (with a line about it in the log); the log no longer grows with garbage.
- Settings: in the locked stand-in (a broken file without a usable copy) the setup assistant appeared, which could have set the lock and the codes anew through the service. The stand-in never counts as a first start any more: no assistant, no first-start notice, also not under Settings › Info, and the shell mode setup does not start there: before its first step it asks CouchHUD again whether the settings can be used, and it stops when CouchHUD refuses to keep the couch open for the setup, with the reason shown instead of running on quietly (before, the refusal was swallowed as "locked" and every step ran anyway). The assistant and the shell mode setup ask every time, not only when the interface starts. A change that is not saved on the stand-in no longer changes it in memory either (before, it held until the next start, a couch lock switched off for example). When `config.json` and every usable copy are missing but traces of an earlier setup are there (a copy of a broken file, a play time account, a wrong-code brake, `parental.key`, a file in place of the data folder, or a data folder that is itself a junction or a link), CouchHUD runs locked as well instead of with open defaults; deleting `config.json` alone is no fresh start any more (remove the whole folder `%APPDATA%\CouchHud` for that). The notice "Settings cannot be used" says so.
- Play time accounts and wrong-code brakes: the `.bak` gets the new state right after every save, as for `config.json` (from the first save on); before, `playtime.json.bak` lagged a save behind, and a restore gave back the time played last. When replacing the `.bak` fails half way (the old one is gone already), the new copy becomes the `.bak` instead of being deleted (for `config.json` the same); temp files a hard reset left next to play time accounts and wrong-code brakes are cleaned up by the next save once they are older than a minute.
- Own start target: a batch file over 1 MB is neither approved nor started, with an honest hint (a start script is a few lines, and so the check holds up no start). SECURITY.md says that the approval covers only the chosen batch file, not the scripts or programs it calls.
- Settings, after the security re-review: in the locked stand-in (`config.json` broken or missing) the couch code, the parents' code and the lock mode still changed in the service, the settings refused the change silently, and the call reported success; service and settings drifted apart. Now the code, the parents' code, the lock mode, parental controls (switch, rules, locked entries, time zone), the shell, the integration level, the service setup and the way back, the Task Manager block and switching automatic sign-in on refuse at once in this state with the notice "Not saved: config.json is damaged …", before any approval and any service call; confirming a lock of 0.4.0 after the Windows sign-in no longer changes the service's lock mode then either. Switching automatic sign-in off stays possible (the safe direction). The way back to normal Windows therefore needs a usable `config.json`; until then, unlocking and PC mode or a restart lead to the desktop. A setup hold from before the fault no longer keeps the couch open. Selecting a service to pause reports an error when nothing was saved, instead of showing the selection as taken.
- Log: warnings and errors still reach the disk at once, but no longer on the thread that writes them (Console.Error is synchronized, the UI would have waited for the disk): coalesced on the thread pool, at most once a second. Closing the log does or waits for a flush still due, and nothing after it.
- Uninstall: cleaning up the shell entries of users who are not signed in opened its own process token through a handle the garbage collector could already have closed (the rights to load their profiles were then missing silently; at worst another object with the same number was hit). It now uses the fixed handle of its own process. The same mistake made the test for restricted pipe clients fail now and then.
- Assistant: the buttons at the bottom (Ask, suggestions, New) wrapped into a second line, the focused one zoomed into its neighbour, and buttons and answers did not line up with the heading and the header. Now one row that never wraps and follows the focused button, with soft faded edges where there is more; the focus is a frame or the skin's own style (Deep Sea white, Greenlight white with the green bar, Neon City yellow, Nova bright) instead of a zoom and a glow the edge cut off. Older messages fade out softly under the header and above the buttons when scrolling instead of being cut hard, and the buttons keep their distance from the hint bar (in Nova they sat on it).
- After the VM test: on a locked couch with parental controls on, "Age rating by" and the areas (settings and assistant) asked the parents' code first and then failed at the couch lock, the code was used up. Now unlock first, then the parents' code, then save; the same for the screensaver and display off (with guarded profiles), the save game settings and a client's download window.
- Time up: "<game> is paused" also showed when the game was not frozen at all (with an anti-cheat, for example). Now the page says it keeps running in the background, and switches once it is frozen.
- Age limit after a change of the Windows region: the row named the new system ("Blocked from age 16 (ESRB)") while the limit was saved under USK. The row and the hint now both name the system it is saved under (in the profile too); the stricter rating of both still counts.
- Setup: "Lock with controller code: Done already" also showed when only the Windows lock of the locked stand-in applied. Now the step stays open.
- Automatic sign-in: switching it off when it was off already reported "The CouchHUD service refused this". Now "Automatic sign-in was already off", but only when Windows shows it off before and after the refusal and nothing of CouchHUD's is stored any more (while the saved password is still there, a refusal stays an error).
- Skins: a skin copied by hand into `%APPDATA%\CouchHud\skins` only appeared after a restart; the chooser reads the list again when it opens.
- PlayStation symbols: the square (□) was smaller than ✕ ○ △, now the same size.
- Age rating on the detail page: when Steam only gives a foreign system, the country is named ("ClassInd (Brazil) 14", "CERO (Japan) …"). On equal ages PEGI still wins; the parental judgement does not change.

**Security (review before 0.5.0)**
- Browser history: with parental controls, switching *Remember recently visited sites* off needs the parents' code for exactly this action (host and page, used up only after the save); the page forgets the recent sites only once the host took the change. Off now also means that the built-in browser's history and download list are deleted, at the latest when it next opens (tried already when switching and when closing).
- Screenshots: after the page reloads CouchHUD refuses until the page has reported whether a code entry or the pairing code is shown; a report counts only once the host took it, and it is repeated every few seconds. Right before the capture it checks again, and a picture during whose capture a code entry showed up is thrown away. At most 20 a minute (only ones really taken count), none with less than 1 GB or unknown free space (each with its own message). A lost "code on screen" report is sent once more after 200 ms. SECURITY.md now says that Windows notifications end up in the picture.
- Home Assistant: signing in over `http://` only on the home network (private and link-local addresses, Tailscale 100.64/10, loopback, `localhost`, names without a dot and ending in `.local`, `.home.arpa`, `.fritz.box`, `.lan`, `.internal`); every other address needs `https://`, otherwise CouchHUD refuses with a hint before any request goes out. An older sign-in over `http://` to a public address ends without retries, and the settings ask to sign in again with https.
- Age ratings: when parents change a limit after the region was switched, the system that applied so far stays checked; only when they choose *Go by …* (in simple mode too) or *Age ratings by* themselves does the new one alone count. Picking the same system again moves no limit.
- PlayStation controllers: when reading stops, a read in flight is cancelled and waited for before its memory is freed.
- Screen capture loads `dxgi.dll` and `d3d11.dll` only from System32. The uninstall (with administrator rights) reads `config.json` only up to 4 MB and not through a link.

**Last VM test before 0.5.0**
- Home Assistant: the error no longer ends in "(Parameter 'baseUrl')"; only the hint itself is shown.
- Screenshots: the "at most 20 a minute" message comes once per burst instead of again and again. Only after 10 seconds without a press may a screenshot be taken again (and, if the minute is still full, the message come once more).
- Guide menu: the menu is no longer see-through; a bright switch behind it shimmered through as a faint circle.
- Power menu in shell mode: restart, shut down and the like are only red in their text and have no red outline of their own, which looked like a second focus (all skins).
- Uninstall: if CouchHUD was the Windows shell, the uninstall asks at the end to sign out and in again; then the normal Windows desktop starts. On purpose the uninstall (with administrator rights) does not start Explorer itself.
- Uninstall, log: if the CouchHUD service is there but cannot be reached (e.g. when called from a network logon, which it refuses on purpose) or is stopped, the log says so instead of "no service".
- Stardust skin (catalog): "Wetter & Smart Home" no longer wraps in the category list, and the guide menu is no longer see-through here either.

## 0.4.3 (2026-10-27)

### Deutsch

**Sicherheit**
- **Fix für 0.4.1 und 0.4.2: Eine Webseite hielt die Couch ohne Eingabe entsperrt.** Der Couch-Browser verlängert die 2-Minuten-Entsperrung, solange jemand ihn benutzt. Als Benutzung zählte aber auch alles, was die Seite selbst tat: neuer Titel, neue Adresse (`pushState`), Verlauf, Ton. Eine Seite, die ihren Titel alle paar Sekunden ändert, hielt so die ganze Couch dauerhaft offen. Jetzt verlängern nur Eingaben eines Menschen: der Controller (Tasten, und der Stick, der den Mauszeiger führt), eine echte Tastatur oder Maus (nicht CouchHUDs eigene Eingaben wie die Controller-Maus, das Aufwecken des Bildschirms, die Tasten für das Nachtlicht oder das Holen in den Vordergrund), die Knöpfe der HUD-Leiste, Text aus der HUD-Tastatur und das Öffnen des Browsers. Was die Seite selbst tut (Titel, Adresse, Navigation, Ton, Vollbild, ihre Dialoge, Downloads), verlängert nichts. Die Warnung „Der Browser schließt sich gleich“ kommt wie bisher 30 Sekunden vor dem Ende; eine Taste verlängert dann. Wer ein langes Video im Browser schaut, drückt also alle zwei Minuten eine Taste, oder schaltet die Couch-Sperre aus.
- Nacharbeiten zum Fix: Das Öffnen des Browsers zählt nur, wenn ein Mensch es im HUD auslöst (Browser-Kachel, Adresse, Link, Website-Eintrag aus Bibliothek oder Menü); ein Link eines Plugins und ein Start über MCP oder den Assistenten verlängern nichts (deren Bestätigung mit A am Controller zählt ohnehin). Eingaben gelten erst eine volle Sekunde nach CouchHUDs eigenen Eingaben wieder als echt (vorher 250 ms). Neue Quelltext-Tests halten fest, wo die Nutzung des Browsers und die Entsperrung gesetzt werden dürfen und dass jede von CouchHUD erzeugte Taste, Mausbewegung und jeder Klick als eigene Eingabe markiert ist.

### English

**Security**
- **Fix for 0.4.1 and 0.4.2: a web page kept the couch unlocked without any input.** The couch browser extends the 2-minute unlock while someone uses it. But everything the page did by itself counted as use too: a new title, a new address (`pushState`), history, sound. A page that changes its title every few seconds kept the whole couch open for good. Now only a person's input extends it: the controller (buttons, and the stick that moves the cursor), a real keyboard or mouse (not CouchHUD's own input such as the controller mouse, waking the display, the night light keys or bringing its window to the front), the HUD bar's buttons, text from the HUD keyboard and opening the browser. What the page does by itself (title, address, navigation, sound, full screen, its dialogs, downloads) extends nothing. The warning "The browser closes soon" still comes 30 seconds before the end; a button press extends then. So whoever watches a long video in the browser presses a button every two minutes, or switches the couch lock off.
- Follow-ups to the fix: opening the browser counts only when a person does it in the HUD (the browser tile, an address, a link, a website entry from the library or the menu); a plugin's link and a start through MCP or the assistant extend nothing (their confirmation with A on the controller counts anyway). Input counts as real again only a full second after CouchHUD's own input (250 ms before). New source tests pin where use of the browser and the unlock may be set, and that every key, mouse move and click CouchHUD makes up itself is marked as its own.

## 0.4.2 (2026-10-27)

### Deutsch

**Controller**
- Code ohne Controller: Die Code-Abfrage (Couch-Sperre, Eltern-Code, Code festlegen im Assistenten und in den Einstellungen) zeigt die zehn Code-Tasten zum Anklicken mit Maus oder Touch und die Tastaturbelegung (Enter = A, Esc/Rücktaste = B, X, Y, Q = LB, E = RB, Pfeiltasten, M = Abbrechen). Gleicher Code, gleiche Prüfung und gleiche Fehlversuchssperre im CouchHUD-Dienst. Ist kein Controller verbunden, steht das dort zusammen mit dem Bluetooth-Zustand; geht der letzte Controller verloren, sagt ein Hinweis, dass Tastatur und Maus auch gehen.
- Bluetooth schaltet sich wieder ein (neue Experten-Einstellung *Bluetooth für Controller automatisch einschalten*, Standard an): beim Start und sobald Bluetooth ausgeht, wenn ein Bluetooth-Controller gekoppelt und kein anderer Controller verbunden ist. Mit Tastatur oder Maus in Benutzung erst nach einem abbrechbaren Countdown von 10 s (nie über einer Code-Abfrage oder der Zeit-vorbei-Seite), im Flugmodus mit Tastatur oder Maus auch beim Start; ohne andere Eingabe immer. Wird Bluetooth von außen immer wieder ausgeschaltet, gibt CouchHUD nach zwei vollen 10-Minuten-Fenstern bis zum nächsten Start auf. Nur das vorhandene Bluetooth wird eingeschaltet (nie koppeln, nie sichtbar machen); deaktiviertes oder fehlendes Bluetooth wird nur protokolliert, Fehlschläge mit wachsendem Abstand und Obergrenze.
- Bluetooth in CouchHUD ausschalten fragt nach, wenn der Controller darüber verbunden ist; mit anderem Controller, Tastatur oder Maus bleibt es danach aus, sonst kommt es wieder.
- In der Code-Abfrage geben Mausrad und Seitentasten keine Code-Tasten ein, gehaltene Pfeile wiederholen nicht; ein Klick neben ein offenes Menü schließt nur das Menü.
- Die Zeit-vorbei-Seite des Jugendschutzes lässt sich jetzt auch mit der Maus bedienen.
- Handbuch: neuer Abschnitt „Controller weg?“.

**CaseHUD**
- CaseHUDs Schnittstelle ist ab Werk aus (CaseHUD: Einstellungen → Schnittstellen). CouchHUD unterscheidet jetzt nicht installiert, nicht gestartet, Schnittstelle aus und Schnittstelle an und zeigt bei ausgeschalteter Schnittstelle auf der CaseHUD-Seite, der Beleuchtung, im Guide-Menü, im Einrichtungsassistenten und in „Was CouchHUD ändert“ den Hinweis *In CaseHUD unter Einstellungen → Schnittstellen die API einschalten*, mit *Schnittstelle einschalten* und *Am PC öffnen*. 403 `api_disabled` wird zu diesem Hinweis, nicht zu einem Fehler.
- Bei Vollintegration bietet die Installationsseite (und die neue Experten-Einstellung *Schnittstelle nach der Installation einschalten*, Standard an) an, die Schnittstelle nach der Erstinstallation für dein Konto einzuschalten; der Assistent hat dafür einen eigenen Schritt, wenn CaseHUD schon installiert ist. CouchHUD ruft dazu `CaseHUD.exe --set-api=on` (bzw. `off` zum Ausschalten) aus dem geprüften Installationsordner unter Program Files in deiner Sitzung auf, nie aus dem Dienst und nie bei Updates; ein Fehlschlag zeigt den Hinweis. Ein- und Ausschalten braucht die entsperrte Couch.
- CouchHUD beobachtet den CaseHUD-Ordner: Port und Token werden bei jeder Änderung neu gelesen, der Live-Status bricht ab, wenn CaseHUD die Schnittstelle schließt, und verbindet sich neu, sobald sie wieder da ist (ohne Ordner oder Watcher alle 5 s nachsehen). `api.json` und `instance.pid` werden streng geprüft (Größe, JSON, Port, Token, Prozesspfad, eigene Sitzung, Startzeit vor dem Schreiben der Datei).

**Sicherheit** (Abnahmetest 0.4.1)
- **Fix für 0.4.1, zwei Wege an der Couch-Sperre vorbei:** (1) Benutzer-Umgebungsvariablen `WEBVIEW2_*` (setzbar von jedem Standardkonto, z. B. mit `setx`) überstimmen die Optionen von WebView2, etwa `WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS=--remote-debugging-port=9222`: Damit konnte jedes Programm die Oberfläche fernsteuern, den eingetippten Code mitlesen und die Brücke aufrufen; andere Variablen setzen einen eigenen „Runtime“-Ordner oder Datenordner. CouchHUD entfernt jetzt beim Start, vor jedem WebView2, alle Variablen, die mit `WEBVIEW2_` beginnen (nur die Namen landen im Log); auch von CouchHUD gestartete Programme erben sie nicht mehr. Die Richtlinien-Schlüssel von WebView2 (`Software\Policies\Microsoft\Edge\WebView2`) liegen auch unter HKCU nur für Administratoren beschreibbar. (2) `--dev` (Fenster, DevTools, keine Wiederherstellungsprüfung) wirkte auch in Release-Versionen und ließ sich über eine Verknüpfung oder einen Autostart-Eintrag setzen. Jetzt nur noch in Debug-Builds oder wenn ein Administrator `HKLM\SOFTWARE\CouchHud` `DeveloperMode=1` (DWORD) setzt; ist der Schlüssel für andere beschreibbar, zählt er nicht. Remote-Debugging für Tests gibt es nur noch über `RemoteDebuggingPort` im selben Schlüssel (zusammen mit `DeveloperMode=1`, nur 127.0.0.1; `scripts/vm-cdp.ps1 -Enable`/`-Disable`).
- WebView2-Runtime: Das Setup installiert die Runtime jetzt immer für alle Benutzer, auch wenn das Konto schon eine eigene (pro Benutzer, in `%LOCALAPPDATA%`) hat; die konnten Programme des Kontos verändern oder vortäuschen. CouchHUD übergibt WebView2 für Oberfläche und Couch-Browser ausdrücklich den Ordner der Runtime aus HKLM, nur unter Program Files (kanonischer Pfad, keine Verknüpfungspunkte); sonst entscheidet wie bisher der Loader. Läuft die Oberfläche auf einer Runtime außerhalb von Program Files, warnen bei eingeschalteter Sperre die Konsolen-, Windows- und Jugendschutz-Einstellungen und raten, das Setup als Administrator erneut auszuführen (kein Startverbot: das endete nur in der Windows-Sperre). Außerdem: Die `WEBVIEW2_*`-Bereinigung löscht die dokumentierten Namen zusätzlich einzeln und wiederholt sich, bis nichts übrig ist; der Entwickler-Schalter in HKLM wertet die rohe Zugriffsliste aus (unbekannte Einträge zählen als Schreibrecht, eine fehlende Liste ebenso) und nur DWORD-Werte; die Runtime-Prüfung gilt nur noch für die WebView2-Runtime unter Program Files, nicht mehr für ganz `C:\Windows`. `scripts/vm-cdp.ps1 -Disable` entfernt `WEBVIEW2_*` auch aus der Umgebung der Couch-Testkonten.
- WebView2-Start: Startet die Oberfläche oder der Couch-Browser auf der Runtime aus HKLM nicht, versucht CouchHUD es einmal mit neu ermitteltem Ordner (ein Update kann ihn gerade ersetzt haben). Wurde das Profil zuletzt von einer neueren Runtime benutzt (`EBWebView\Last Version` höher als die Runtime), legt CouchHUD es einmal beiseite (umbenannt in `….newer-runtime-<Zeit>`, nie gelöscht) und startet erneut. Ohne Couch-Sperre und Jugendschutz folgt danach einmal die Wahl des Loaders mit Warnung; mit Sperre oder Jugendschutz nie: dann Desktop-Rückfall mit Windows-Sperre und Logzeile (fail closed). Lässt sich die tatsächlich laufende Runtime nicht prüfen, warnen die Einstellungen „Laufzeit nicht prüfbar“; die Warnung erscheint jetzt auch bei Jugendschutz ohne Couch-Sperre und berücksichtigt den Couch-Browser. Der Entwickler-Schalter liest die Zugriffsliste direkt über `RegGetKeySecurity`. Vor dem Beiseitelegen wird die gescheiterte WebView entsorgt und bis zu 3 Sekunden auf das Ende ihres Browserprozesses gewartet; es bleiben höchstens zwei beiseitegelegte Profile (das älteste wird gelöscht, ein Ordner hinter einer Verknüpfung nie). Bei Couch-Sperre oder Jugendschutz sperrt der Desktop-Rückfall Windows.
- Update von 0.4.0: Die alten Aufgaben der Aufgabenplanung (`CouchHud\<SID>\Policy-*`, als SYSTEM, vom Benutzer startbar) kann kein Benutzer mehr starten. Der CouchHUD-Dienst setzt bei jedem Start jede Aufgabe im Ordner `CouchHud` auf SYSTEM und Administratoren, übernimmt die Sperre eines angemeldeten Kontos als seine eigene (sie bleibt an) und löscht erst danach dessen alte Aufgaben und Dateien. Abgemeldete Konten folgen bei ihrer nächsten Anmeldung; bis dahin bleiben Sperre und (gesperrte) Aufgaben. Schon das Setup schränkt die Aufgaben ein, bevor der neue Dienst läuft, und der Dienst tut es bei jedem Start, bevor er seine Pipe öffnet. Ausgenommen ist nur `Autologon-off` (schaltet nur die automatische Anmeldung aus); sie bekommt ausdrücklich nur SYSTEM, Administratoren und Starten für ihren Benutzer. Als Beleg zählt nur, was ein Administrator angelegt hat; Sperr-Werte ohne diesen Beleg übernimmt der Dienst nicht. Bei der Übernahme vergisst der Dienst alle Codes und den Sperrmodus des Kontos. Ein Administrator bestätigt die Sperre beim ersten Öffnen einmal per UAC, und zwar genau „den eben eingegebenen Code“ oder den „Windows-Modus“; alle anderen bisherigen Codes, auch ein Eltern-Code, gelten danach nicht mehr. Das heißt: Couch-Code und Eltern-Code von Konten mit 0.4.0-Restdateien werden verworfen, es braucht eine Administrator-Bestätigung, und den Eltern-Code legt man danach neu fest. Passt der eingegebene Code nicht zum gespeicherten, kommt UAC erst, nachdem man im Dialog „Als Administrator bestätigen“ gewählt hat; der Code wird dann der neue Code der Sperre. Bis dahin, und schon bevor der Dienst die Sperre übernommen hat, geben weder Couch-Code noch Windows-Anmeldung noch Eltern-Code sie frei, der Sperrmodus ändert sich nur mit Administrator-Bestätigung (nie auf „keine Sperre“), und einzelne Codes lassen sich gar nicht setzen, ändern oder entfernen. Die Deinstallation gibt auch noch nicht übernommene Sperren von 0.4.0 frei.
- Ein Code, der in einer gesperrten Windows-Sitzung eingegeben wird, ergibt keine Freigabe mehr; der Versuch zählt für die Wartezeit nach Fehlversuchen. Auch ein Windows-Entsperren oder eine Administrator-Bestätigung wird in einer gesperrten Sitzung nicht eingelöst (und nicht verbraucht).
- Couch-Sperre: Der Wechsel vom Code zur Windows-Anmeldung sagt vorher, dass der Couch-Code gelöscht wird. Zurück zum Code legt man gleich einen neuen fest und bestätigt ihn einmal mit der Windows-Anmeldung (mit Jugendschutz mit dem Eltern-Code); eine Administrator-Bestätigung ist dafür nicht mehr nötig.
